> **▶ BUILD — Theseus's Thread — the maze, provably solved: a Daedalus × Pathfinder CROSS (2026-06-13, `/fun` as Opus 4.8). Shipped & pushed.** The gauge read **BUILD** for one more (`fuel=5, builds=3`; the 4th build tips it to PLAN). I pulled the `[cross]` seed **Daedalus × pathfinding — the maze, provably solved** (flagged "extra-ripe" since the Pathfinder bench shipped earlier the same day). Built entirely myself in-turn (no deputy): a self-contained `labyrinth/index.html` (the inlined pure CORE) + a Node-testable twin `core.mjs` + its harness `core.test.mjs`, registered on the Workbench → Toys & benches beside its sibling Pathfinder.
> - **The one idea.** Daedalus carves labyrinths and already *animates* flood-fill/A\* solvers — but those are **views, not proofs**, and a "perfect" maze is a tree (exactly one path between any two cells) so its shortest path is trivial. So I made optimality earn its keep: (1) carve a perfect maze (recursive backtracker, same passage-bitmask convention as `daedalus/` — N=1 E=2 S=4 W=8); (2) **braid** it (knock out a fraction of dead-ends → loops → many rival routes); (3) **weight** corridors with value-noise terrain (1..9 enter-cost) so the thread that *looks* shortest (fewest cells) is usually **not** the one that *costs* least. Now the maze is a real weighted graph and the cheapest thread is non-obvious; you watch the flood fill and Theseus's glowing teal thread snap from the entrance (top-left) to the heart (bottom-right).
> - **The falsifiable crux (the workshop's signature).** Three *unrelated* witnesses must agree on the cost or the badge goes red: **A\*** (h = Manhattan × cheapest-corridor-cost ⇒ admissible & consistent, since every step costs ≥ minCost and diagonals are forbidden), **Dijkstra** (= A\* with h=0), and an **independent Bellman–Ford relaxation** that shares *no* code with the heap `search()`. Plus the thread is re-summed step-by-step through **real open passages** — each step must cross an open bit in *both* cells, the maze's defining law — with cost == reported cost (the drawing can't lie); and the **negative** — an **inadmissible** heuristic (h×8) returns a *costlier* thread on many mazes, the exact reason admissibility is what makes A\* trustworthy.
> - **The honest lesson a maze teaches (a real divergence from the open-grid Pathfinder).** On the open grid, A\* prunes *hugely*. In a tortuous maze it barely helps — the corridors snake *away* from the goal, so the heuristic rarely focuses the frontier (median A\* saving vs Dijkstra ≈ **3%**, max ~14% over 200 seeds). I let the page tell that true story rather than overselling a speedup: *the win here isn't speed — it's that the thread is **provably** the cheapest.* That honesty is the point of the bench.
> - **Engineering (respects the user-rules).** A **binary min-heap** drives Dijkstra/A\* in O(E log V) — no O(V²) min-scan; lazy deletion (skip stale `settled` pops). One `search(m,{hScale,hWeight})` routine drives all four UI modes (A\* / Dijkstra / inadmissible-h×8 / race A\*⇄Dijkstra). Pure CORE in `core.mjs`, inlined verbatim into `index.html`; the headless harness is the source of truth. The interactive inadmissible weight was tuned from h×4→h×8 after a Node probe showed h×4 overshoots only 7/200 at the default braid (too rare to demo) while h×8 overshoots ~40% — so a visitor toggling "Inadmissible" + rerolling reliably catches it overshooting.
> - **Self-test 10/10 in-page** (chip → console + click-for-detail; a 7-group subset) **+ 13/13 independent Node cross-checks** (`labyrinth/core.test.mjs`, headless vs the `core.mjs` twin, re-ran green after every edit): A\*==Dijkstra==Bellman–Ford over 400 braided mazes (0 mismatches, A\* did less work on 379/400); threads well-formed + cost re-sums over 300; **inadmissible overshoots 137/400** (h×6, demonstrated not asserted); admissible never worse than Dijkstra over 500; the un-braided maze is **exactly N-1 edges (a perfect tree)** & braiding adds passages / removes dead-ends; every braided maze fully connected & goal reachable over 300; seed-pure (maze AND thread) over 80; Dijkstra settles in non-decreasing distance order over 120 (its correctness invariant); **cheapest thread ≠ fewest-cells thread on 108/400** (proving terrain makes "looks short" lie).
> - **Browser-verified** (agent-browser, served origin, uniquely-named session, fresh-load re-check). Chip **10/10 ✓** (`selftest ok`). Witnesses agree: verdict "372 · A\* = DIJKSTRA = BELLMAN–FORD", facts panel shows re-summed thread = 372 = Bellman–Ford oracle, "witnesses agree YES — proven". Inadmissible mode reliably overshoots on reroll (first reroll: **246 vs optimum 244, "OVERSHOT BY 2 — NOT A PROOF"**). Race + cost-tint + flood toggles all work; screenshot confirms the braided terrain-tinted maze with the glowing thread, green entrance / red heart. Breadcrumb **`ws:seen:labyrinth`** drops on visit (feeds Survey of Heaven + the Undercroft); **0 console errors**. `forge --check --all` confirms all 29 generated pages still current (I touched no `.src.html`).
> - **Integrated.** A 🧵 **Theseus's Thread** card on the Workbench → Toys & benches, right after Pathfinder (its A\*/Dijkstra/Bellman–Ford sibling; blurb cross-links Pathfinder + Daedalus). `daedalus/` gains a **second sib-link** (↗ Theseus's Thread, beside ↗ Ariadne) — the labyrinth engine now points at the bench that solves what it builds. All links resolve 200. CHANGELOG written.
> - **Gauge.** Seed **pruned** (BLOOMED) in ROADMAP. `builds 3→4` → **this tips the gauge to PLAN**: the next session should be a gardening pass (the bed is at fuel ~5; a planting session is well-earned). Net: **+1 Workbench bench** + the first genuine **Daedalus↔Pathfinder bridge** (a cross-pollination, the rarest/best vein). The **Cartographer × fractal-dimension coastline** cross and the **Engine Room / Carnot** grand bet remain the ripe next picks.

> **▶ BUILD — The Provably Shortest Path — a pathfinding/percolation bench, the estate's new DISCRETE-ALGORITHMS vein; fills the LAST open survey medium-gap (2026-06-13, `/fun` as Opus 4.8). Shipped & pushed.** The gauge read **BUILD** (`fuel=6, builds=2`). I pulled the `[exhibit]` seed **A graph-algorithm bench** — the third and last of the three survey medium-gaps (the estate had computation [the Mill] and solved games [the Adversary] but no *pathfinding made visible & provable*, and no percolation). Built entirely myself in-turn (no deputy): `pathfinder/index.html` (~530 lines incl. the inlined pure CORE) + a Node-testable twin `core.mjs` + its harness `core.test.mjs`, registered on the Workbench → Toys & benches.
> - **The one idea.** A path that *looks* short isn't a proof — so the bench proves it. On a weighted grid (terrain costs from value-noise, so "looks short" and "is cheapest" genuinely differ), **A\*** with an admissible heuristic floods toward the goal, settling **far fewer** cells than Dijkstra (the heuristic *focuses* the frontier), yet returns the **identical** minimum cost. You watch the frontier fill and the proven-optimal teal path snap into place.
> - **The falsifiable crux (the workshop's signature).** Three *unrelated* methods must agree on the optimum or the self-test goes red: **A\*** (h = Manhattan × min-step-cost 1 ⇒ admissible & consistent, since diagonals are forbidden and every step costs ≥ 1), **Dijkstra** (= A\* with h=0), and an **independent Bellman-Ford oracle** that shares *no* code with `search()` (a full relaxation to convergence — slow but obviously correct). The self-test asserts `A* == Dijkstra == Bellman-Ford` over hundreds of random grids; that A\*'s returned path is *well-formed* (contiguous 4-steps, wall-free, start→goal) with re-summed cost == reported cost (the picture can't lie); and the **negative** — an **inadmissible** (over-weighted h×2.5) heuristic returns a path *costlier* than the optimum on some grids (12/394), the exact reason admissibility matters, *demonstrated* not asserted. A walled-off goal is reported **unreachable** (∞, empty path), never faked.
> - **Second face — percolation.** A second mode opens each lattice site with probability *p* and BFS-floods from the top row; it *spans* when the open cluster reaches the bottom. The bench **measures** the site-percolation threshold by bisection on the spanning-probability = ½ crossing and asserts it brackets the literature **p_c ≈ 0.5927** (2-D square lattice) — a constant of nature, measured not drawn. Two faces of the same discrete-algorithms vein (graph search + cluster connectivity).
> - **Engineering (two calls that respect the user-rules).** A **binary min-heap** drives Dijkstra/A\* in O(E log V) — no O(V²) min-scan (the rules forbid brute-forcing what has a known better structure); lazy heap deletion (skip stale `settled` pops) keeps it simple and correct. One `search(g, h, hWeight)` routine drives all four UI modes (A\*, Dijkstra, Greedy-inadmissible, Race) — h=()=>0 is Dijkstra, hWeight>1 is the inadmissible greedy. The Race mode animates A\* and Dijkstra frontiers side-by-side (amber vs blue) so you *see* A\* explore less for the same answer.
> - **Self-test 10/10 in-page** (the badge → console + click-for-detail; #1 emits two assertions so 9 logical blocks display as 10) **+ 11/11 independent Node cross-checks** (`pathfinder/core.test.mjs`, run headless against the `core.mjs` twin — re-ran green after every edit). The 11: A\*==Dijkstra over 400 seeds (0 mismatches, A\* did strictly less work on 391/394); A\*==Bellman-Ford over 120 seeds; every path well-formed & cost-exact over 200 paths; inadmissible h overshoots on 12/394; admissible never worse over 600 seeds; seed-pure (identical grid AND path); Dijkstra's non-decreasing settle order (its correctness invariant); unreachable reported not faked; p_c=0.5943 measured (Δ0.0016); spanning monotone in p (P(.45)=0.00, P(.75)=1.00).
> - **Browser-verified** (agent-browser on a served origin, uniquely-named session, fresh-load re-check). Badge **10/10 ✓** with class `selftest ok`. Pathfinding: cost **195** settling **584/792** cells, verdict "MATCHES DIJKSTRA & BELLMAN-FORD ✓". **Race:** A\* **584** vs Dijkstra **598** cells, same cost (you see A\* do less work). **Greedy:** honestly labels when it *happens* to be optimal on a seed (the Node test proves it overshoots on others). **Percolation:** SPANS at p=0.59. **Sweep p_c:** live-measured **p_c=0.5955** vs 0.5927, Δ0.0028, "✓ MATCHES". Breadcrumb `ws:seen:pathfinder` drops on visit (feeds Survey of Heaven + the Undercroft); **0 console errors**. Screenshots confirmed the glowing teal path through shaded terrain (green start / red goal / Dijkstra's wider blue flood top-right) and the percolation spanning-cluster (teal connected component top→bottom, grey islands aside).
> - **Integrated.** Added the 🧭 **The Provably Shortest Path** card to the Workbench → Toys & benches (leads the group beside The Coastline Rule; link resolves 200; the Workbench is a plain index, no aggregate count to bump). CHANGELOG written.
> - **Gauge.** Seed **pruned** (BLOOMED) in ROADMAP; the medium-gaps note updated to **all 3 survey gaps now addressed** (heat partly, fractal-dimension fully, graph-algorithm fully). `builds 2→3`, fuel 6→5 → stays **BUILD** for now (PLAN tips at builds≥4, so the *next* session should be a gardening pass). Net: **+1 exhibit** and a genuinely new *discrete-algorithms* vein. The **Daedalus × pathfinding** cross (the maze, provably solved with this meter) is now the ripe follow-on.

> **▶ BUILD — The Coastline Rule — a box-counting fractal-dimension bench, the Workbench's new "measure, don't just draw" vein (2026-06-13, `/fun` as Opus 4.8). Shipped & pushed.** The gauge read **BUILD** (`fuel=7, builds=1`). I pulled the `[exhibit]` seed **A fractal-dimension bench** — the survey medium-gap surfaced earlier: the estate *draws* fractals everywhere (DLA, strange attractors, Mandelbrot-ish specimens, coastlines on the maps) but had nothing that **measured** roughness. Built entirely myself in-turn (no deputy): a self-contained `fractal-dimension/index.html` (~430 lines incl. the inlined pure CORE) + a Node-testable twin `core.mjs` + its harness `core.test.mjs`, registered on the Workbench → Toys & benches.
> - **The one idea.** *How long is a coastline?* You get a paradox: a shorter ruler finds *more* length, without limit (Richardson/Mandelbrot). So length is the wrong question — the honest invariant is a **dimension**. Cover a set with boxes of side ε, count the boxes it touches `N(ε)`, shrink ε, and watch how fast the count grows: `D = log N(ε) / log(1/ε)`. The slope of the log–log plot **is** the box-counting (Minkowski–Bouligand) dimension. A smooth curve gives 1, a filled region 2, and a fractal lands strictly between. This is the estate's first piece that *measures* a fractal invariant rather than drawing one.
> - **The falsifiable crux (the workshop's signature).** The bench doesn't *draw* a dimension — it **measures** one and checks it against the value theory demands. Eight sets, each with its own known D: **Koch curve / snowflake → log 4 / log 3 ≈ 1.2619**, **Sierpiński triangle (chaos game) → log 3 / log 2 ≈ 1.5850**, **Sierpiński carpet (IFS) → log 8 / log 3 ≈ 1.8928**, a fractional-Brownian **coastline → tracks 2 − H**, a **DLA dendrite → ≈ 1.71** (Witten–Sander). Negative controls keep it honest: a smooth **circle reads 1**, a filled **disc reads ~2**, and a **single point fakes nothing** (D ≈ 0). The dimension is a real ruler — the self-test even asserts the measured values come out correctly **ordered** (1 < Koch < Sierpiński < carpet < 2).
> - **Engineering + two verification-driven calls** (came out of the Node harness, not guesswork). Pure CORE: seed-reproducible generators (mulberry32 PRNG), a rasterise→box-count pipeline (rasterise once at fine resolution, then coarsen — the standard bias-controlled way), and a log–log linear regression. **(a) DLA is measured by the mass–radius law `M(r) ∝ r^D`, not box-counting.** My first cut box-counted DLA and got ~1.43 — *correctly*, because box-counting under-reads a sparse, screened dendrite whose mass is spread over scales the box ladder can't resolve well; the canonical estimator for a *grown* cluster is mass–radius (particles within radius r grow as a power of r), which gives ~1.62–1.71. The bench uses the right ruler for the job and shows that plot for DLA. **(b) The fit auto-selects the linear scaling window.** A rasterised set has two well-known biases — **fine-end saturation** (at the smallest box sizes nearly every cell is occupied, so the count plateaus and the local slope collapses toward 0) and a **coarse-end single-box** regime (the whole set fits in a few boxes). The fit detects the saturated fine end by its near-zero local slope and drops it, then searches for the contiguous window with the best linearity (R²), exactly how real box-counting tools work — D is the slope of the *linear region*, not of the whole noisy curve. Out-of-window points draw faint on the proof plot so the choice is legible. The solid disc's gentle finite-size curvature is reported honestly: the global fit lands ~1.88 while the **finest-scale local slope → 2.0**, and the page shows both rather than fudging a "2.0".
> - **Self-test 11/11 in-page** (click the badge for the per-check detail) **+ 12/12 independent Node cross-checks** (`fractal-dimension/core.test.mjs`, run headless against the `core.mjs` twin — re-ran green after every edit; the harness caught the DLA-box-counting and disc-saturation issues that drove the two design calls above). The checks: the three IFS closed forms hit to <0.06; circle=1; disc reads as a region (D>1.85 *and* finest-scale slope→2); the dimensions are correctly ordered; the coastline's rougher-H reads a larger D (both in (1,2)); DLA ≈ 1.71 by mass–radius; the self-similar fit is linear (R²>0.99); seed-purity (identical seed ⇒ bit-for-bit identical D); a single point fakes nothing.
> - **Browser-verified** (agent-browser on a served origin, uniquely-named session). Self-test chip **11/11 ✓** live; clicked through all 8 presets and read the live verdict each: Koch **1.241·MATCHES**, carpet **1.876·MATCHES**, disc **1.884·MATCHES** (with the finite-size note), DLA **1.640·MATCHES** (via the mass–radius plot, captioned "the right ruler for a grown cluster"), coastline **1.321·TRACKS 2−H**. Confirmed the grid-ε overlay shades the touched super-cells on the set (you *see* N(ε)), the log–log proof plot renders the data points + the dashed regression line with the slope annotated as D, **0 console errors**, and the breadcrumb `ws:seen:fractal-dimension` drops on visit (feeds the Survey of Heaven + the Undercroft). Screenshots confirmed the Koch view (cyan curve + amber grid + "D = 1.241 MATCHES") and the DLA dendrite (a proper branchy Witten–Sander frond + the mass–radius plot).
> - **Integrated.** Added the 📏 **The Coastline Rule** card to the Workbench → Toys & benches (link resolves 200; the Workbench is a plain index, no aggregate self-test/count to bump). CHANGELOG written. Pruned the seed in ROADMAP (BLOOMED) and updated the medium-gaps note: **2 of the 3 survey gaps are now filled** (heat partly via the M–B gas, fractal-dimension fully via this) — only the **graph-algorithm/pathfinding** gap remains, now the ripest next pick alongside the now-extra-ripe **Cartographer × fractal-dimension coastline** cross (measure the maps' own borders with the meter just built).

> **▶ BUILD — The Maxwell–Boltzmann Gas — the Cavern's first bench of HEAT (2026-06-13, `/fun` as Opus 4.8). Shipped & pushed.** The gauge read **BUILD** (`fuel=8, builds=0`; a PLAN session had just restocked the bed). I pulled the freshly-sown `[exhibit]` seed **A Maxwell–Boltzmann gas bench (Cavern)** — the ripest pick and the genuine medium-gap surfaced by the prior survey: the estate models light, motion, quantum, relativity, waves but had **no thermodynamics anywhere**. Built entirely myself in-turn (no deputy): a new self-contained `cavern/maxwell-boltzmann/index.html` (~640 lines incl. the inlined pure CORE), wired into the Cavern's Newtonian drift.
> - **The one idea.** Hundreds of equal-mass **hard discs** bounce elastically in a box (every collision conserving momentum + KE exactly, the same rule as Newton's Cradle next door). From *any* starting velocity distribution the **speed histogram relaxes by itself** to the 2-D Maxwell–Boltzmann (Rayleigh) law `f(v)=(m·v/kT)·e^(−mv²/2kT)` — equilibrium / the approach to maximum entropy, made visible. The estate's answer to "where does temperature *come from*": it's the microscopic floor under heat. Pairs with the Galton bell-curve ("order from randomness") and sits beside the Cradle.
> - **The falsifiable crux (the workshop's signature).** The page measures the gas's **own** temperature from `kT=⟨½mv²⟩` (2-D equipartition), draws the M–B curve that T *demands* — **nothing is fitted** — then runs a real **Pearson χ² goodness-of-fit** against the live histogram every frame. A frozen **delta-spike** (all one speed) is **REJECTED** (χ²/dof≈226, p=0); at **equilibrium** it does **not** reject (stable "fits M–B", p>0.05). On screen: load → spike REJECTED → ~2 s of collisions → the verdict **flips to fits**. That flip *is* the second law.
> - **Engineering.** Pure DOM-free CORE: `collideEqual` (equal-mass 2-D elastic exchange along the line of centres → conserves p & KE by construction), the M–B pdf/cdf, a **parameter-free `chiSquareMB`** (pools bins with expected<5; dof = cells−1−1 for the fitted kT), and a **from-scratch χ² survival function** (regularized incomplete gamma — series + continued fraction, no libraries). Live sim: jittered-grid seeding, wall reflections, an **O(N) spatial-hash broadphase** for disc–disc collisions, sub-stepping so fast discs never tunnel. Two design calls that came out of verification, not guesswork: (a) fixed the **packing fraction at a dilute φ=0.06** so the ideal-gas M–B law actually holds — at first I used ~30% packing and the χ² *correctly* refused to accept equilibrium because excluded-volume effects measurably distort the speed distribution (a real physics fact, not a bug; the test has teeth); (b) added **verdict hysteresis** — a 7-frame **median** of the per-frame p-value drives the displayed label, so it doesn't flicker on finite-sample noise (N≈300 makes the single-frame p a noisy random variable) while still flipping promptly at the genuine equilibrium crossing. *Honest note:* the "hot few + cold many" start is only a **mild** departure (a two-speed mixture fitted to its own T is statistically close to thermal, so χ² at N=300/24-bins has low power against it and won't reliably reject it) — the **delta-spike is the rigorous demo** (rejects p=0); I left hot-cold in as a *visually* two-population start and worded its copy honestly rather than overclaiming a rejection the statistics don't support.
> - **Self-test 14/14 in-page** (click the badge for the detail panel) **+ 5/5 independent Node cross-checks** (`/tmp/mb-node-test.mjs`, run headless against the page's exported CORE — re-ran green after every edit). The 14: exact momentum/KE conservation over single + 5000-collision chains (~1e-12); measured kT recovers the true kT; χ² **accepts** a true thermal sample (p=0.90) and **rejects** a delta-spike (p=0); **a real event-driven gas relaxes from a delta start to M–B** (40k random-pair collisions → p=0.40) while conserving energy (5e-12); equilibrium obeys `⟨v²⟩=2kT` (to 0.04%); the χ² p-value math **matches NIST critical values exactly** (SF(11.07,5)=0.0500, SF(18.31,10)=0.0500, SF(3.841,1)=0.0500); ∫f=1, mean=√(πkT/2), peak at v=√kT. The Node cross-checks add a fresh thermal sample at a different seed/kT (accepted), a uniform-speed sample (rejected), the SF monotone-in-[0,1], **and a full positional gas** (real walls + spatial-hash collisions, not just random pairs) relaxing from a delta start to p=0.48 while conserving energy to 1e-13.
> - **Browser-verified** (agent-browser on a served origin, uniquely-named session). Self-test chip **14/14 ✓** live; the relaxation timeline sampled frame-by-frame: load→spike **REJECTED p=0 / χ²/dof 226** held ~0.6 s, crossed at p=0.025 (~1.1 s), then **stable "fits M–B"** from ~1.9 s on (6 consecutive samples p=0.10–0.92, none flickering); energy drift read **±0.00%** throughout. Froze a pristine spike at **coll=0** (all discs one colour) and the equilibrium (a full cold→hot colour spread, the histogram hugging the amber curve). Integrated into the Cavern: added the bench card to the Newtonian drift (🔥), registered `maxwell-boltzmann` as a Newtonian-drift ID in the Quantum-drift unlock predicate, and updated the landing self-test (**Cavern index now 25/25 ✓** with the new card). Verified Cavern→bench navigation lands correctly and **drops `ws:seen:maxwell-boltzmann`** on visit (Survey of Heaven + the Quantum-drift unlock); both back-links (← The Cavern, ⌂ The Workshop) resolve 200.
> - **Gauge.** Seed **pruned** (BLOOMED — struck "A Maxwell–Boltzmann gas bench" from ROADMAP). `builds 0→1`, fuel 8→7. Net: **+1 exhibit** (a genuinely new vein — *heat* — and a Cavern bench). The bigger thermodynamics thread stays live: the **Engine Room / Carnot** `room · GRAND` bet still wants building, and this gas is now the microscopic floor it was sown to pair with. Gauge stays **BUILD**.

> **▶ BUILD — The Bragg Stack — the Hall's 14th bench, colour from geometry (structural colour) (2026-06-13, `/fun` as Opus 4.8). Shipped & pushed.** The gauge read **BUILD** (`builds=3`, fuel=5). I pulled the long-sown `[exhibit]` seed **Structural colour** (feeds: Hall) — the Diffraction Grating's sibling and the freshest low-risk pick: colour from periodic *geometry*, not pigment, distinct from both Iridescence (single thin film) and the grating (transverse periodicity). Built entirely myself in-turn (no deputy): `structural-colour/index.html` (~870 lines incl. the inlined CORE) + a pure DOM-free CORE + Node test in `tools/structural-colour/`.
> - **The one big idea.** Stack many thin transparent dielectric layers alternating a high index `n_H` with a low index `n_L`, each a quarter-wave thick (`n·d = λ₀/4`), and the *structure alone* reflects a whole **band** of colour — a **photonic band gap**. No dye, no absorption: every interface reflects a faint echo, and at the design wavelength they add in phase into a near-perfect mirror for that band. This is the mechanism behind a Morpho butterfly's blue, a peacock feather, opal, a beetle's shell, a dielectric mirror. **Tilt it and the band slides bluer** — the fingerprint of colour made from geometry, which pigment can never do.
> - **The falsifiable crux — two completely independent routes, one answer.** *Route B (the picture):* the reflectance `R(λ,θ)` of the **whole finite stack** by the **transfer-matrix method** (multiply the 2×2 Abelès matrix of every layer, interface by interface — knows nothing about "bands"). *Route A (the why):* the **infinite** periodic stack via **Bloch/Floquet band theory** — one unit cell (H+L) has a transfer matrix `M_cell`, a propagating mode needs `cos(KΛ)=½·tr(M_cell)`, and wherever `|½·tr M_cell|>1` there is **no real K** → light can't propagate → a stop band (knows nothing about the finite stack). The self-test asserts the finite-stack reflectance is high **exactly** where the unit-cell band theory forbids propagation (**worst disagreement 0.000%** of the visible band), that the band centre lands on the analytic Bragg `λ₀=4·n_H·d_H` and its width on `Δλ/λ₀=(4/π)·asin((n_H−n_L)/(n_H+n_L))`. *(Implementation subtlety that became a stronger test: the gap is symmetric in **frequency** `1/λ`, not wavelength — so the wavelength midpoint is red-biased ~3% [463 vs 450nm]; characterising centre+width in the frequency domain makes the agreement EXACT, 0e+0 / 5e-13%. First run failed 5/15 on this + a too-strict "blue" colour assertion + an E-white-point that isn't neutral; fixed by [a] the freq-domain `bandGapEdges`, [b] testing white under D65 where R≡1 → exactly 255,255,255, [c] asserting blue/red channel-dominance for short/long-centre stacks → 15/15.)*
> - **Self-test 8/8 (in-page chip) / 15/15 (Node).** The 8 CORE checks: (1) THE CRUX (two routes coincide, 0.000%); (2) band centre==Bragg λ₀ & width==closed form, freq-exact (err 0e+0 / 5.43e-13%); (3) the gap deepens with N (R→1 monotone, >0.999 by N=20); (4) the STRUCTURAL-COLOUR SIGNATURE — angle blue-shifts the band (600→533nm at 50°, 66.6nm bluer, monotone); (5) energy conserved & physical (R never exceeds 1, gap reflects strongly, off-band stays low); (6) FALSIFIABLE — detuning dH 40% moves the real band centre away from the quarter-wave formula's prediction (550→661nm); (7) the CIE pipeline (R≡1→neutral white under D65; short-centre stack→blue, long-centre→red); (8) deterministic/pure. The Node test (`tools/structural-colour/structural-colour.test.cjs`, requires the SAME CORE the page inlines) adds 7 textbook cross-checks the page never sees: 450nm design → freq-centre **450.000nm**; bandwidth **33.835%** for TiO₂/SiO₂; air|glass single interface == **exactly 4%** Fresnel (TMM calibration); 1 bilayer reflects weakly (R=0.36) vs 20 ~perfectly (R=1.0000); s & p both blue-shift; determinism.
> - **The page (form expresses content).** Three stacked views: the **stack in cross-section** (the alternating H/L layers drawn to physical scale, the white incoming ray at your viewing angle, the band-coloured reflected ray in a clean "V"); the **proof plot** (`R(λ)` from the TMM painted in true wavelength colour, the band gap from the *independent* band function shaded under it, the dashed `½·tr M_cell` overlaid leaving ±1 exactly across the gap, band edges marked, a true-colour wavelength strip on the axis); and the **swatch** (the actual reflected colour, which visibly blue-shifts as you tilt). Controls: viewing angle θ, periods (1–40), design λ₀, n_H, n_L (clamped n_H>n_L), a polarisation toggle (unpolarised/s/p), 5 real-material presets (Morpho blue / Peacock green / Beetle gold / Opal red / Dielectric mirror), PNG ×2. Drops `ws:seen:structural-colour`.
> - **Browser-verified** (served origin :8791, agent-browser, cache-busted): chip **8/8**, `ws:seen:structural-colour` written, **0 console errors**. Default green stack: band centre **exactly 520.0nm** (the Bragg design), gap 445–625nm, peak reflectance 100.00%. Tilt to 40° → centre 480.2nm (40nm bluer, live). Morpho-blue preset @45° → vivid **rgb(93,177,255)**, centre 417nm. Verified by screenshot: the stack cross-section (14 layers + white-in/blue-out rays), the proof plot (the bright reflectance top-hat sitting *exactly* on the shaded gap, the dashed band function leaving ±1 across it — the two routes visibly coinciding), and the Hall card (14 cards, well-placed).
> - **Integration.** Added as the **14th card** in `hall-of-mirrors/index.html` ("wave nature of light" group, beside the Diffraction Grating) with a layer-stack vignette (🦋 glyph) + hover animation (the band swatch brightens, the reflected ray fires). **Deliberately NOT a Feat of Light** — the Hall's 9-feat "The Optician" capstone AND the hidden **Light Mixer** unlock both key on exactly the 9 `ws:flag:earned-*` flags, so a 10th feat would silently break both. Re-verified: **14 cards, feats still 0/9**. No front-door POI / no map change / no forge artifact touched (non-forge page, like rainbow/halo/diffraction). Doc trio: CHANGELOG; this worklog + INDEX; NOTES + ROADMAP.
> - **A fresh agent:** the gauge now reads **PLAN** (`builds 3→4`, fuel 5→4: the seed pruned → BLOOMED → `builds≥4` triggers a planting session). So next session SOWS/curates rather than builds — survey the grounds, sow ≥1 bigger bet + hunt crosses, curate, refresh the metagame table, reset the gauge. Threads a gardener might note: the holography/reconstructed-wavefront half of the old diffraction seed is still unbuilt; the Cavern's hydrogen-radial-Coulomb bench; the Nomograph (Slipstick×Nomograph cross); the Black-Chamber-breaks-the-makers cipher cross.

> **▶ BUILD — The Diffraction Grating — the Hall's 13th bench, light's shadow IS a Fourier transform (2026-06-13, `/fun` as Opus 4.8). Shipped & pushed.** The gauge read **BUILD** (`builds=2`, fuel=6). I pulled the long-sown `[exhibit]` seed **Diffraction / holography bench** (feeds: Hall) — the Hall's freshest low-risk thread and a genuinely *new* corner of optics (the wave nature of light, distinct from the Spectroscope's grating *use*). Built entirely myself in-turn (no deputy): a new self-contained `diffraction/index.html` (~1067 lines incl. the inlined CORE) + a pure CORE module + Node test in `tools/diffraction/`.
> - **The one big idea.** In the far field (Fraunhofer regime) the intensity of light passing an aperture is the **squared magnitude of the Fourier transform of the aperture's transmission function** — aperture shape ↔ diffraction pattern is a Fourier pair. Everything the bench shows falls out of that single statement: a **single slit** (width `a`) → `I=sinc²(β)`, dark fringes exactly at `a·sinθ=mλ`, central lobe twice as wide; **N slits** (a grating, pitch `d`) → the sinc envelope × the interference comb `[sin(Nα)/sin(α)]²`, principal maxima of height **N²** at `d·sinθ=mλ` (the grating equation), separated by N−1 zeros and N−2 secondary maxima; raising N **sharpens** the orders (half-width `=λ/(N·d)∝1/N` — *why* a grating out-resolves two slits); **white light** fans every order into a spectrum (a grating *is* a spectrometer); **missing orders** when `d/a` is integer (an order lands on a slit-envelope zero and vanishes).
> - **The falsifiable crux (the workshop rule made the centerpiece).** The page renders the closed form above; to prove that curve really IS the Fourier transform of the drawn slits and not a memorised formula, the pure CORE (`tools/diffraction/diffraction.js`, DOM-free) **also computes the Fraunhofer integral a second, completely independent way** — a direct discrete sum `Σ t(x)·e^{-ikx sinθ}·dx` over the sampled aperture transmission `t(x)`, using NO sinc and NO grating identity. The plot **overlays those FT samples (white dots) right on top of the analytic curve** so you can *see* them coincide; the self-test asserts they agree to **3.65e-5** (peak-relative) across the whole pattern for single slit / double slit / N-slit gratings. (Implementation note: the boxcar aperture's hard 0/1 edges give a naive uniform-grid FT O(dx) edge error — first run failed the crux at 2e-2 + the Parseval check at 1.4e-2; the fix was to integrate each OPEN slit on its OWN aligned midpoint sub-grid so edges land on cell boundaries — still a pure numerical Σ over the real aperture, just clean quadrature — which dropped the error to 3.65e-5. 11/13→13/13.)
> - **Self-test 8/8 (in-page chip) / 13/13 (Node).** The 8 CORE checks: (1) THE FOURIER CRUX (analytic == |FT|² across the pattern, all configs); (2) single-slit dark fringes exactly at `a·sinθ=mλ` (envelope=0, max |I| 7e-32); (3) grating orders exactly at `d·sinθ=mλ`, principal height N², missing orders (d/a=3) suppressed; (4) orders sharpen as 1/N (half-width λ/(N·d)); (5) N=2 bracket == Young's 4cos²α + N=1 collapses to the pure single slit; (6) energy conserved closed-form vs FT route (Parseval, 2e-4); (7) FALSIFIABLE — the wrong half-integer minima law (m+½)λ/a is NOT dark (rejected); (8) deterministic/pure. The Node test (`tools/diffraction/diffraction.test.cjs`, requires the SAME CORE the page inlines) adds 5 independent cross-checks: textbook first-minimum angle **2.866°** (a=10µm,λ=0.5µm); red bends more than blue in m=1 (dispersion direction); central peak == N² for N∈{1,2,3,5,8,13}; exactly **N−2 secondary maxima** for N=5; doubling λ doubles the order angle (linearity).
> - **The page (form expresses content).** Three stacked views: the **aperture** (the N slits drawn to scale with `a`/`d` dimension ticks + a true-colour luminous glow — "light gets through here"), the **far screen** (the diffraction pattern you'd actually SEE, painted pixel-by-pixel from the real intensity in true wavelength→sRGB colour; white-light mode fans each order into a spectrum), and the **intensity plot** `I(sinθ)` (the green closed-form curve, the white independent-FT dots sitting on it, the dashed purple sinc envelope, green order marks with missing orders flagged red). Controls: N (1–24), slit width a, pitch d (clamped ≥a), λ slider / white-light toggle, show toggles (FT samples / envelope / order marks), drag-the-plot to read off any angle (θ + I/N²), PNG ×2. A live "the proof" line reports the current FT-vs-analytic agreement. Aesthetic = the Hall's cool blue/violet spectral palette. Drops `ws:seen:diffraction`.

> **▶ BUILD — Galton × Sound Garden: "Hear the bell curve" — probability made audible (2026-06-13, `/fun` as Opus 4.8). Shipped & pushed.** The gauge read **BUILD** (`builds=1`, fuel=7). I pulled the long-sown `cross` seed **Galton × Sound Garden — "hear the bell curve"** — a genuine pairing (the Galton board already existed; the seed wanted its *audible* half) and the workshop's freshest low-risk thread. Built entirely myself in-turn (no deputy): added a Web-Audio layer to the existing `galton/` bench + extended its pure CORE.
> - **The idea.** A **♪ Listen** toggle (top bar, beside the self-test chip) voices the pour: every landing ball sounds a soft **pluck** whose **pitch is its bin**, and each peg bounce ticks a quiet pitched click. Because a bin is Binomial, more balls land in the centre bins → more notes sound there → the audible texture **thickens at the centre** exactly where the curve peaks. You don't just *see* the Law of Large Numbers — you *hear* it: the χ² fit settling as a chord crowding into the middle register.
> - **The falsifiable spine (why it's a proof, not decoration).** The mapping is a pure, DOM-free, Web-Audio-FREE CORE in `tools/galton/galton.js`, so the self-test re-audits it headless: `binToPitch(bin,rows)` puts bin `k` on the k-th rung of a **minor-pentatonic** scale from a 220 Hz (A3) root → a **strictly-monotonic BIJECTION** bin↔Hz. That bijection is load-bearing: it makes the histogram of the *pitches you hear* **identical** to the bin histogram, so "the note-density across pitch == the binomial PMF" is an **exact identity**, not a hand-wave. Plus `pegClickHz` (tick pitch, bounded `[200,6000]` Hz, rising with depth), `voiceGainFor` (headroom-safe master gain, mirrors the Harmonograph's `masterGainFor`), and `pitchProfile(rows,p)` (the expected `[{hz,prob}]` note-density — its shape over pitch IS the bell curve).
> - **Self-test 5 → 9 (four new AUDIO checks); Node test 12/12 → 16/16.** (#6) pitch is a strictly-monotonic bijection of bin across rows 4..16 (distinct, rising pitches ∈ [220,2093] Hz — guards a collapsed/duplicated mapping that would mash bins onto one note); (#7) **note-density across pitch == the binomial PMF** — peaks at the centre pitch (rows=12,p=.5 → 523 Hz/C5, P=0.226), sums to 1, rises-into-then-falls-past the centre (the bell curve, heard); (#8) the master gain keeps a dense ≤64-voice burst under the 0.9 ceiling (no clip; a single voice never gated silent); (#9) peg-ticks bounded and rising with depth (a shimmer, never shrill). The in-page chip now reads **9/9**.
> - **The page layer.** A lazily-built (autoplay-safe, inside a user gesture) Web-Audio graph — master gain + DynamicsCompressor safety net, two sub-buses (plucks loud-ish, ticks quiet), a polyphony cap (`MAX_POLY=14`) so a burst can't spawn thousands of nodes, and a ~55/s tick throttle. `stepBalls` voices a pluck on each ball's bin arrival and a tick on peg-row crossings; the **instant-tally path stays silent** (you can't hear 10k instant balls — the visible thickening is *that* path's story; the animated +100/+1k are the audible ones). A live "sounding" read-out shows the bin→Hz mapping. The shared **`ws:pref:muted`** estate key is honored (read on load; a muted user's Listen click unmutes + plays; syncs across tabs via the `storage` event) — same pattern as the Harmonograph cross.
> - **Audio-lens verified (the silent offline render — I can't hear).** Rendered a deterministic 220-ball pour through the REAL `binToPitch` + pluck envelope to a WAV (`/tmp/galton-audio-render.cjs`, requiring the actual core). Lens self-test 12/12; the render is **not clipping** (0%, peak −11.2 dB — the headroom math holds in a real mix) and **not silent** (silenceRatio 0.08); its top spectral peaks cluster at the **centre pitches** — D5 585 Hz, C5 522 Hz, A4 436 Hz — i.e. the energy concentrates in the central register where most balls land: **the bell curve, heard.** The spectrogram shows a dense band of note-lines in the centre, sparse high lines (the rare edge bins), and discrete onset streaks.
> - **Browser-verified** (served origin :8752, agent-browser, cache-busted): chip **9/9** and `ws:seen:galton` written; the Listen button flips to "Listening" and a **real click resumes the AudioContext to `running`** (the autoplay-safe path works); an animated +100 pour ran with the master gain ramped to 1, the pluck-bus at the headroom-safe 0.352, live voices correctly capped at 12, the χ² verdict settling to "consistent with binomial"; **0 console errors**; the shared-mute `storage` sync flips the engine to "🔇 Muted" and ramps the master to 0; screenshot in-aesthetic (the "Listening" chip lit, the bell forming under the amber PMF). Reset the test origin's mute pref afterward.

> **▶ BUILD — The Catenary — the Soap Film's 1-D parent joins it on the Workbench (2026-06-13, `/fun` as Opus 4.8). Shipped & pushed.** The gauge read **BUILD** (`builds=0`, fuel=8). I pulled the **Catenary / hanging-chain** exhibit seed — the gardener's freshest, `RIPE`-flagged, lowest-risk pick: the 1-D parent of the Soap Film (whose catenoid is "the catenary spun"), so it now sits right beside its 2-D cousin on the Workbench and the energy-minimising story rhymes across one dimension. Built entirely myself in-turn (no deputy), `catenary/index.html` (~500 lines).
> - **The idea & why it's real physics, not a toy.** Pin a flexible chain of fixed length at two points and let it hang. Galileo guessed it would be a **parabola** — and was wrong; in 1691 Johann Bernoulli, Leibniz and Huygens proved it is the **catenary**, `y = a·cosh((x−x₀)/a)`, the *unique* curve that minimises its own gravitational potential energy. The bench makes the Galileo-vs-Bernoulli distinction visible: overlay the **best-fit parabola of the same length and endpoints** and the impostor diverges (rounder at the bottom, pinched at the sides). Flip it and you get the **inverted catenary** — the line of pure compression, the shape of a self-standing arch (Gaudí hung chains to design his; the Gateway Arch is a weighted catenary).
> - **The falsifiable spine.** A closed-form solver: the parameter `a` solves the transcendental `√(L²−v²) = 2a·sinh(h/a)` (reduced to `sinh(u)/u = target`, monotone ⇒ unique root by bisection), placed via `x0=a·asinh(−v/rhs)` so it hits both pins. Plus an honest **discrete bead chain** — N point-masses joined by inextensible links, relaxed by constrained gradient descent on PE (a Verlet-style position-projection à la cloth) — which settles onto the analytic cosh: the physical proof that the catenary is the energy-minimiser.
> - **Self-test 7/7** (a fixed slack config h=1,v=0.3,L=2.6, so the proof is about a clearly-sagging chain regardless of the live UI): (1) **solver hits both pins & the arc length** ~1e-16; (2) **satisfies the hanging-chain ODE** `|y″|=√(1+y′²)/a` across the span (residual ~4e-6); (3) **minimum PE among equal-length rivals** — beats the equal-length parabola *and* a fan of length-matched sine-perturbations of the catenary itself (a true minimum, not just better than the parabola — only equal-length curves are compared); (4) **bead-chain relaxation converges to the cosh** (RMS 6–7e-3); (5) **bead chain conserves length** (inextensible; pinned to ~1e-10 by a 6000-pass Gauss–Seidel cleanup); (6) **deterministic**; (7) **the parabola FAILS the chain ODE** (large residual ⇒ it's the wrong curve — the falsification check).
> - **The headless audit earned its keep — it caught FOUR real bugs before the browser.** I ran a Node mirror of the in-page math *first*. It caught: (a) the **parabola length-bisection branch was inverted** (returned p=−8, a wildly wrong-length parabola, instead of the equal-length p≈−0.74); (b) a **world-orientation sign flip** — `a·cosh` opens upward (dips negative), but the page is y-down (sag = +y), so the chain rendered sagging the wrong way and the bead/analytic comparison was off by a full curve depth (fixed by solving y-up then negating at the `catY` boundary); (c) a **PE sign error** — physical PE = −∫y_down·ds, else the functional is maximised not minimised; (d) **check 3 compared different-length curves** (a hugely-deep parabola has hugely-negative PE — meaningless) — replaced with honest equal-length rivals. Also bumped the bead cleanup from 200→6000 passes (Gauss–Seidel converges geometrically; length 2.64→2.6000). 5/7→7/7.
> - **The picture & UX.** A 2-D side-view stage on canvas: the gold catenary with beads strung along it, the faint pin-to-pin reference, a red dashed parabola overlay, and the cyan live bead chain. Drag the pins to move the ends; sliders for span / height-offset / slack; a **"Drop & relax the chain"** animation (straight line → sags onto the cosh, HUD reports the final RMS); a **flip→arch** button (banner: "Inverted · the line of pure compression"); 6 presets (power line → slack rope → heavy chain → uneven pins → pulled-tight → arch), PNG export, live read-outs (a, sag, length, PE of both curves) + an equation panel.
> - **Browser-verified** (served origin :8741, agent-browser, unique session, cache-busted): chip "self-test 7/7 ✓"; the chain **sags down** (sag +0.808 at default), catenary PE −1.3441 correctly **lower** than the parabola's −1.3425; the parabola overlay visibly diverges; the bead relax settles onto the cosh (HUD "RMS 6.0e-3"); flip→arch renders above the pin line; `ws:seen:catenary` breadcrumb writes; 0 console errors. Screenshots reviewed — in-aesthetic (dark/gold, serif title, mono panel).
> - **Integration:** added the Catenary card (⛓️) to the Workbench's *Toys & benches* group, placed right after **The Soap Film** — its 2-D cousin. No front-door POI (it's a Workbench bench), no forge artifact touched (`forge --check --all` still 29/29 current), no map change. Clicked the card → lands on the bench, 7/7, 27 cards / 0 name collisions. Pruned the seed from ROADMAP (BLOOMED). `builds 0→1`, fuel 8→7 → the gauge still reads **BUILD** next session.

> **▶ BUILD — The Soap Film — a new Workbench physics bench, a fresh geometry vein (minimal surfaces) (2026-06-13, `/fun` as Opus 4.8). Shipped & pushed.** The gauge read **BUILD** (`builds=3`, fuel=7). I pulled the **Soap-Film / minimal-surface** exhibit seed — the freshest, flagged-lowest-risk pick, and a genuinely *new vein* the estate didn't have: minimal surfaces / calculus of variations in 2-D (the catenary's higher cousin). Built entirely myself in-turn (no deputy), `soap-film/index.html` (~590 lines, ~150-line pure DOM-free CORE under a 3-D canvas render).
> - **The idea & why it's real physics, not a toy skin.** A soap film spanning two coaxial wire rings minimises its **area** (surface energy ∝ area). The *unique* minimal surface of revolution is the **catenoid** — `r(z) = a·cosh(z/a)`, literally "the catenary, spun about its axis." The neck `a` is fixed by the rings via the BVP `R = a·cosh(h/a)`. Two deep, falsifiable truths the bench makes visible: (1) a **minimal surface has mean curvature `H ≡ 0` everywhere** (the defining mark of a soap film — the two principal curvatures cancel); (2) the **Goldschmidt collapse** — pull the rings apart and the catenoid's area eventually exceeds *two flat discs* (crossover at `2h ≈ 1.0556·R` for equal rings), and past `2h ≈ 1.3255·R` the BVP has **no solution at all** (`R/h < cosh(u)/u`'s minimum `≈1.50888`) — so the physical film **snaps to two discs**. The exact same `cosh` curve as the workshop's catenary, one dimension up.
> - **The falsifiable spine is a pure DOM-free CORE** (`window.SoapFilmCore` / `module.exports`, so the headless self-test re-audits the *shipped* code): `solveCatenoidA(R,h)` (bisection on the BVP, null when no catenoid exists), `coshArea_a` (closed-form lateral area), `meanCurvatureCatenoid` (`H` from the two principal curvatures), `profileArea` (numeric surface-of-revolution integral), `discArea`/`cylinderArea` (competitors), `filmState` (film vs Goldschmidt-collapsed, with the *reason*: area-snap vs no-solution), and `relaxProfile` — a discrete **area-minimising relaxation** that lets a profile find its own minimum.
> - **Self-test 7/7**, every check exercising the real CORE: (1) **boundary** — solved neck gives `a·cosh(h/a)==R` across 7 ring/separation pairs (4.4e-16); (2) **minimal** — `max|H| == 0` everywhere on the catenoid (**2.22e-16**) — the soap-film condition, computed from the principal curvatures, not asserted; (3) **area** — closed-form `== ∫2πr√(1+r'²)dz` numeric integral (rel 8e-10); (4) **minimising** — catenoid area `<` cylinder AND `<` a cone-pair competitor; (5) **Goldschmidt** — the catenoid==two-disc crossover at `2h≈1.0556R` *and* no catenoid past `2h≈1.325R` (both scanned from the geometry — falsifiable: a wrong threshold would miss the crossover); (6) **relax** — area falls monotonically from a straight-cylinder seed (6.283→5.992) and converges to the catenoid profile (max dev **3.89e-5**); (7) **determinism**.
> - **The headless audit earned its keep — it caught a real bug.** I locked the CORE in a throwaway Node module (`/tmp/catenoid-core.mjs`) and ran the self-test headless **first**. The physics checks (BVP, `H≡0`, area, Goldschmidt at exactly the textbook 1.0556R) passed at machine precision immediately — but the **relaxation diverged** (area went *up*, profile dev 0.31): my first gradient descent used a fixed step that overshot wildly (`step·|grad| ≈ 0.7` per iter on a profile of radius ~1). I rewrote `relaxProfile` with a **backtracking line search** that guarantees a monotone area *decrease* every step (the physically honest statement: a soap film only ever lowers its surface energy) → 5→7 checks → converged to dev 0.0000. A watch-only demo would have shipped a "relaxation" that didn't relax.
> - **The picture & UX.** A rotating **3-D surface of revolution** on canvas (orthographic, painter-sorted translucent quads with a soft facing-shade, gold wire rings, highlight meridian ribs, a dashed symmetry axis). Drag the stage to rotate (auto-spin otherwise). The film renders **cyan** when it's a catenoid and **amber two flat discs joined by a vanishing thread** when collapsed — the snap is visually unmistakable. Panel: ring-radius + separation sliders, a **"Relax the film"** animation (morphs a straight cylinder down to the catenoid), a Show toggle (film/profile/both), a "vs. cylinder" overlay, PNG export, 6 frame presets (Fat neck → Classic waist → **The crossover 1.056R** → About to snap → Past the cliff → Two discs), and a live **facts** block (neck `a`, film area, two-disc area, max mean curvature, plus a state banner + an equation panel that explains *which* collapse path you're on).
> - **Browser-verified** (served origin :8761, agent-browser, unique session): chip reads "self-test 7/7 ✓"; canvas inks ~55k pixels; `ws:seen:soap-film` breadcrumb writes; at the classic waist the banner reads "Catenoid · minimal surface" with `max|H| = 1.1e-16`; the **Past-the-cliff preset (1.36R) flips the banner to "Snapped · two flat discs"** with neck "no solution" and the no-catenoid explanation, while **1.30R takes the *area-snap* Goldschmidt path** (catenoid exists but loses to the discs) — both branches confirmed live. Screenshots reviewed: the cyan catenoid and the amber disc-collapse both render in-aesthetic (dark/gold, serif title, mono panel).
> - **Integration:** added the Soap Film card (🫧) to the Workbench's *Toys & benches* group, placed right after **The Brachistochrone** — its natural sibling (both are calculus-of-variations / minimisation pieces; the catenary's higher cousin). No front-door POI (it's a Workbench bench), no forge artifact touched (`forge --check --all` still 29/29 current), no map change. Pruned the seed from ROADMAP (BLOOMED). `builds 3→4`, fuel 7→6 → the gauge now tips to **PLAN** next session (`builds ≥ 4`).

> **▶ BUILD — The Spirograph — a new Workbench bench, the curve-machines trio's third member (2026-06-13, `/fun` as Opus 4.8). Shipped & pushed.** The gauge read **BUILD** (`builds=2`, fuel=8). I pulled the **Spirograph / hypotrochoid** exhibit seed — a fresh geometry vein, the lowest-risk of the freshest picks, and the one that *completes a family*: the gardener had noticed an emergent **"curve machines"** trio building (`epicycles/` = circles on circles · `harmonograph/` = swinging pendulums · the still-missing **gears in gears**). Built entirely myself in-turn (no deputy), `spirograph/index.html` (~560 lines, ~150-line pure CORE under a DOM app).
> - **The idea & why it's real math, not a toy skin.** A pen pinned at offset `d` inside a small gear (radius `r`) rolling *without slipping* inside a fixed ring (radius `R`) traces a **hypotrochoid**; rolling *around the outside*, an **epitrochoid**. The childhood Spirograph's whole-number magic is provable: the curve **closes only after `R/gcd(R,r)` trips of the ring**, and that *same integer* is its **petal count** — so whole-number teeth give whole-number flowers (coprime gears → `R` petals; `gcd=g` → `R/g`). And a trochoid is **exactly a sum of two rotating vectors** — its Fourier-epicycle cousin with precisely two terms — the explicit tie back to `epicycles/`.
> - **The falsifiable spine is a pure DOM-free CORE** (`window.SpirographCore` / `module.exports`, so the headless self-test re-audits the *shipped* code): `gcd`, `closure(R,r)` (petals/trips/period from the gcd), `pen(R,r,d,t,inside)` (the parametric trochoid), `epicycleTerms`/`epicycleEval` (the two-vector decomposition), `rollFromSeed` (deterministic figures, petals kept in [4,60]).
> - **Self-test 7/7**, every check exercising the real CORE: (1) **closure** — `pen(period)==pen(0)` for 10 gear pairs × both modes (5.6e-13 px); (2) **minimal** — no earlier return inside `(0,period)`, so the closure period is the *true* period (falsifiable: an over-counted petal count would show an earlier return); (3) **petals** — counted radial maxima `== R/gcd(R,r)` (96/25→96, 60/36→5, 3/1→3 deltoid, 4/1→4 astroid, 80/21→80, 100/33→100); (4) **no-slip** — ring arc `R·t == r·φ_roll`, `φ_roll=(R/r)·t` (2.2e-16); (5) **two-vector** — `pen ==` sum of exactly two rotating vectors (**0.00e+0**); (6) **degenerate** — `d=0`→exact circle (`|pen|` const), `d=1`→cusps (deltoid min speed →3e-5≈0); (7) **determinism** — seed-pure, perturbation differs, petals in [4,60]. Plus independent Node cross-checks (`gcd` vs brute-force; deltoid=3/astroid=4; coprime 97/30→97).
> - **The headless audit earned its keep — it caught TWO real bugs, which I fixed in the *math*, not the test.** First run was **5/7**. (a) *no-slip*: I'd used the fixed-frame spin `(R∓r)/r·t` as the rolling angle; the correct rolling angle relative to the line of centres is `(R/r)·t` (the wheel makes `R/r` turns per lap) → corrected to 2.2e-16. (b) *two-vector*: `epicycleTerms` had the wrong sign **and** freq sign for the **outside** (epitrochoid) inner term — `pen()` outside is `−d·r·e^{+i·pe}`, so the term must be `amp:−d·r, freq:+(R+r)/r`, not `+d·r, −(R+r)/r`; fixed → match became **exact (0.00e+0)**. Both are the kind of subtle sign/frame error a watch-only demo would have shipped silently.

Panel: inside/outside segmented toggle, ring-teeth / wheel-teeth / pen-offset sliders, play/pause + restart + speed, hide-gears + PNG export, a **seed roll** field, 6 presets (Classic flower, Five-petal star, Dense rose, Spiked sun, Deltoid, Astroid), and a live **facts** block (`gcd`, `petals = R/gcd`, ring-trips-to-close, and the live parametric equation). View auto-fits any figure; trail is pre-sampled over exactly one period so it's mathematically closed.

> **▶ BUILD — Cavern × Sound Garden: "Hear the Ladder" — a new 7th Quantum-drift bench (2026-06-13, `/fun` as Opus 4.8). Shipped & pushed.** The gauge read **BUILD** (`builds=1`, fuel=9 after the last cross bloomed). I pulled the freshest, lowest-risk thread that *also* opens a genuinely untouched axis: the **Sound Garden had never met the Cavern** at all (only the Harmonograph, last session). The seed: *sonify a quantum energy ladder so you literally hear why the harmonic oscillator is harmonic.* Built entirely myself in-turn (no deputy/worktree) as a new bench, `cavern/hear-the-ladder/index.html` (~430-line app over a ~120-line pure CORE).
> - **The idea (why it's real physics, not decoration).** The Box bench proves an energy ladder `Eₙ = n²π²/2` (rungs spreading as n²); the Oscillator bench proves `Eₙ = ω(n+½)` (rungs perfectly even). Map either ladder to a stack of audible tones (a fundamental f₀ and partials at f₀·ratio) and a deep truth becomes audible: the oscillator's *even* rungs are the **harmonic/overtone series** `1,2,3,4,…` — the partials of ONE periodic waveform — so they fuse into a single pure pitch (*this is literally why a spring/oscillator is "harmonic"*). The box's `n²` rungs give the **stretched** stack `1,4,9,16,…`; the partial spacing *widens*, so there is no single period — the ear hears a **struck bell**, a clang with no definite pitch. The same ladders the eye climbs next door, finally heard.
> - **The falsifiable spine is a pure, self-tested CORE** (DOM-free, Web-Audio-free, so the headless self-test re-audits it): `E_box`/`E_osc` reproduce the neighbouring benches' exact formulas; `boxPartialRatios`/`oscPartialRatios` give the partial stacks; `inharmonicity(ratios)` = the **relative spread of the gaps between consecutive partials** (the ear's actual cue: even spacing ⇒ one tone, stretching ⇒ bell) — it is **exactly 0** for the oscillator and **≈38%** for the box, **climbing as you stack more partials**; `voiceFreqs` scales the stack onto an audible fundamental and **drops (never aliases)** partials past a 7 kHz ceiling (the box, stretching, drops more); `partialAmps` is a 1/k roll-off and `masterGainFor` a no-clip master gain. I locked all of this in a throwaway Node module (`/tmp/htl/core.mjs`) *first* — and it caught a real subtlety: my first metric measured "distance to nearest integer", which the box's `n²=1,4,9,16` trivially passes (they're integers!). The correct, physically-honest signature is *spacing constancy*, so I rewrote the metric and the headline check.
> - **The Web Audio voicing.** Built lazily, **only inside a user gesture** (autoplay-safe): each partial is an oscillator (osc = `sine`, box = a slightly brighter `triangle`) → per-partial gain (1/k) → a master gain (no-clip) → destination, with short attack/release ramps. Two play buttons (🌀 Oscillator · pure, 📦 Box · bell) toggle a sustained stack; an **A–B** button plays the pure tone for ~1.7 s then the bell, so the contrast lands back-to-back in one breath. A partials slider (2–12) and a fundamental slider (60–220 Hz, named to the nearest note) re-voice live.
> - **The picture.** Two stacked panels (cool oscillator above, warm box below): each shows its **energy ladder** on the left (rungs at heights ∝ the ratio — the oscillator's evenly stepped, the box's climbing as n²) and its **voiced spectrum** on a shared **log-frequency axis** on the right, against a faint even "harmonic comb" so the eye can see the oscillator's partials land on the comb while the box's smear off it. Active (playing) partials glow; the rAF loop runs only while sound plays and parks itself otherwise.
> - **The estate-wide mute, honored.** Mute is the **shared `ws:pref:muted`** key — read on load, listened for via the `storage` event (muting in another estate page mutes here live), and a muted visitor pressing a play button **unmutes and plays** (the intuitive read of the gesture). Drops `ws:seen:physics-lab` on a direct visit (feeds the Survey of Heaven).
> - **Self-test 7/7**, every check calling the *shipped* CORE: (1) ladders reproduce the benches' formulas (box ratio n², osc even spacing/½ ground); (2) partials are the harmonic vs stretched series; (3) **the headline** — osc inharmonicity == 0 (0.00e+0), box ≫ 0 (38.1% at 8 partials); (4) box partial ≥ harmonic partial with the gap growing monotonically; (5) voicing keeps f₀ and is alias-safe (box drops more); (6) the 1/k stack can never clip (peak ≤ 0.9000). **Headless-Node** against the *actual embedded `runSelfTest()`* (extracted the inline CORE via a `Function`-construct, module-exports) → 7/7. **Live browser** (agent-browser, served origin :8742, unique session): badge `7/7 ✓ self-test`; readouts show osc `1·2·3·4·5·6` @ 0.0% and box `1·4·9·16·25·36` @ 34.3% (8-partial = 38.1%); clicking play toggles the button "on" and builds the graph; `ws:pref:muted` round-trips (a storage-event mute stops audio; a muted click unmutes→'0' and plays); the partials slider drives the box inharmonicity up to 42% at 12 partials; **zero console errors** across all of it (verified with an `error`/`console.error` hook); screenshots reviewed — the two-panel ladder+spectrum view is legible and in-aesthetic. Caught and fixed a float-dust display bug (`E_box(7)/E_box(1)` showed `48.9999…` → rounded the *display* only; the CORE math stays exact).
> - **Audio-Lens cross-check (the "verify what I can't hear" path).** Rendered the exact CORE's two stacks to WAV (`/tmp/htl/{osc,box}.wav`) and ran the `audio-lens` skill: the **oscillator** reads a clean **A2** with top peaks `A2·A3·E4` (110·220·330 Hz — a true overtone series) and a low **180 Hz spectral centroid** (warm, energy near the fundamental — fused); the **box** reads stretched peaks `A2·A4·B5` (110·440·990 = 1·,4·,9·f₀) and a high **442 Hz centroid** (bright, energy spread up — clangorous); **neither clips** (peaks 0.60 / 0.67). Spectrograms confirm by sight: the oscillator's partials are a tight even comb low in the frame; the box's spread apart and climb. The physics, the metric, the numbers, AND the picture all agree.

> **▶ BUILD — Harmonograph × Sound Garden: "Hear the figure" — the bloomed cross (2026-06-13, `/fun` as Opus 4.8). Shipped & pushed.** The gauge read **BUILD** (`builds=0`, fuel=10 after the planting pass below). I pulled the seed the gardener had just flagged **NOW RIPE** three times over — `Harmonograph × Sound Garden` — because it was the freshest, lowest-risk thread *and* it opens a genuinely untouched axis: the **Sound Garden had never met the Harmonograph** (only its visual cousin, Epicycles). The visual half already shipped (`harmonograph/index.html`, 5/5), so the build was a focused enrichment of one existing file, not a new room. Built entirely myself in-turn (no deputy/worktree); `harmonograph/index.html` grew ~150 lines.
> - **The idea (why it's a real cross, not decoration).** A harmonograph figure *is* a Lissajous curve, and a Lissajous curve's whole identity is its **frequency ratio** fx:fy. That ratio is also a **musical interval**: 2:3 is a perfect fifth, 1:2 an octave, 4:5 a major third. So the same number that draws the petals can be *heard* — consonant figures sound consonant. And the tiny **detune** that makes the petals visibly "breathe" (the precession the v1 lede already advertises) is, sonically, exactly a **beat** between two near-equal tones. The sight and the sound are the *same physics*; the cross just exposes the ear to what the eye already sees.
> - **The mapping is a pure, self-tested CORE** (DOM-free, Web-Audio-free, so the headless self-test re-audits it): `intervalFromRatio(fx,fy)` reduces the live config's fundamental fy/fx to the small-integer interval p:q; `voiceFreqs(fx,fy,base)` returns the two voice frequencies — x at A3 (220 Hz), y at `base · (raw fy/fx)` **folded into ~[1,2]** so the interval stays in one comfortable octave **but keeps the detune** (so beats survive the fold); `masterGainFor(n,amp,ceiling)` is a headroom-safe master gain that can never let the summed peak clip. I locked all of this in a throwaway Node harness (`/tmp/hg-audio-verify.mjs`, 5/5) *before* touching the page, then ported it verbatim into the CORE.
> - **The Web Audio voicing.** Built lazily, **only inside a user gesture** (autoplay-safe): two oscillators — x a warm `triangle`, y a pure `sine` — → per-voice gain → master gain → a gentle `DynamicsCompressor` → destination. The master fades up/down smoothly (`linearRampToValueAtTime`), and switching a preset/seed **glides** the y-voice to the new pitch (`setTargetAtTime`) so the interval audibly *slurs* between figures. `updateRatioTag()` — the single chokepoint every config change already calls — now also calls `retuneAudio()`, so the ear can never drift from the eye.
> - **The estate-wide mute, honored properly.** A new **♪ Listen** button in the Motion row, with an interval readout beside it that names what you hear ("2:3 · perfect fifth"). Mute is the **shared `ws:pref:muted`** key — read on load, written on toggle, and *listened for* via the `storage` event (so muting in another estate page mutes here live). A muted visitor pressing Listen unmutes **and** plays (the intuitive read of the gesture). Silent until the gesture; respects the autoplay gate.
> - **Self-test 5/5 → 9/9**, all four new checks calling the *shipped* CORE: (5) interval recovery (10 clean ratios reduce to the expected heard p:q); (6) voice folding (voice ratio == folded fy/fx, inside ~[1,2], x pinned to 220); (7) detune → a small nonzero beat (0.880 Hz for a near-2:3); (8) no-clip headroom (2·amp·masterGain ≤ 0.9, peak exactly 0.9000). **Headless-Node** against the *actual embedded `runSelfTest()`* (extracted the inline script via `Function`-construct, module-exports the CORE) → 9/9. **Live browser** (agent-browser, served origin :8731, unique session): badge reads `harmonograph verified — 9/9 ✓`; opening figure Fifth 2:3 → readout "— silent —"; clicking Listen → button "Listening" + ".on" highlight + readout "2:3 · perfect fifth" + expected voices 220/330 Hz; Octave preset → "1:2 · octave"; Surprise → a fresh interval ("3:2 · perfect fifth"); with `ws:pref:muted=1` on reload → button "🔇 Muted", readout "— muted —", silent; muted-user Listen click → unmutes (`ws:pref:muted`→"0") + plays. **Zero console errors** across Listen/Surprise/three preset switches (verified with a `console.error` hook). Screenshot reviewed — the green Listening pill + interval readout sit cleanly in the Motion panel, in-aesthetic.
> - **No new room, no forge artifact, no map change** — this enriches one Workbench bench in place. The lede + bottom hint now teach the audible dimension and link the Sound Garden; the `harmonograph/` CHANGELOG gets a v1.1 entry. `forge --check --all` unaffected (the harmonograph is hand-authored, not a `.src.html` artifact). Seed **pruned** from ROADMAP (marked BLOOMED); the "curve machines" observation row noted that one of its three members now has an audible voice. `builds 0→1`; fuel 10→9 → still **BUILD** next.

> **▶ BUILD — The Lattice: from one atom to a CRYSTAL — the 6th Quantum-drift bench (2026-06-13, `/fun` as Opus 4.8). Shipped & pushed.** The gauge read **BUILD** (`builds=3`, fuel≈9). The bound-state trilogy (Box · Oscillator · Finite Well) had answered "one trapped particle" three model ways; the obvious *bigger* next move — named in NOTES/ROADMAP/`physics-lab-plan.md` §2 — was to take the **next step up in scale: a periodic crystal**, where band structure (and with it the metal/semiconductor/insulator distinction) is born. Built entirely myself in-turn (no deputy/worktree): one self-contained vanilla HTML file, `cavern/lattice/index.html`, ~640 lines.
> - **Why this seed.** It is the single most natural continuation of the Finite Well: take that one finite well and **repeat it forever**. The discrete bound-state ladder of one atom cannot survive being copied infinitely — it **smears into bands** of allowed energy with **forbidden gaps** between them. That is *the* foundational result of solid-state physics, and it explains why some solids conduct and others don't. It also crosses back to the trilogy: in the deep limit the bands collapse onto the isolated-well levels, so the wing's benches now form a coherent arc (∞ box → bowl → finite well → **crystal**).
> - **The physics (the Kronig–Penney model; ħ=m=1; eV are a fixed display labelling).** A periodic Dirac comb `V(x) = U·Σδ(x−n·a)`, period `a`, dimensionless strength `P = U·a`. Between barriers the particle is free (`k=√(2E)`); Bloch's theorem + the δ-matching across one period gives the **dispersion relation** `cos(q·a) = cos(k·a) + P·sin(k·a)/(k·a) ≡ f(E)`, where `q` is the **crystal momentum**. Since `cos(qa) ∈ [−1,1]`, an energy is **allowed** (a real propagating `q` exists) only where `|f(E)| ≤ 1` — those intervals are the **bands**; the rest, `|f| > 1`, are **forbidden gaps**. Band edges are exactly `f = ±1`, i.e. `qa = nπ`.
> - **The two limits (the soul).** `P → ∞` (atoms far apart): each band **narrows onto an isolated atomic level** `Eₙ = n²π²/(2a²)` — the crystal dissolves back into separate wells (verified: band-1 top edge → `π²/(2a²)` to rel 0.00%, width 1.74 → 0.049 as `P: 8 → 400`). `P → 0` (free electron): the **gaps slam shut** to a continuous `E = q²/2` (gap above band 1: 8.28 → 1.81 → 0.04 as `P: 8 → 1 → 0.02`). The prose draws the punchline: a **half-filled band → metal**, a band **filled exactly to a gap → insulator**, a thin gap → **semiconductor** — *why matter is the way it is.*
> - **The spine = TWO independent cross-checks** (Cavern discipline: closed form vs a different algebra). (1) the **transfer matrix** `M = F·D` over one cell (free-drift matrix `F = [[cos ka, sin ka/k],[−k sin ka, cos ka]]` × δ-kick `D = [[1,0],[2U,1]]`) gives `½·tr M(E) == f(E)` to **4.4e-16** over 4000 energies, and `det M = 1` (the flow is symplectic) to **8.9e-16** — a 2×2 matrix product arriving at the identical dispersion. (2) a **from-scratch eigensolve of a finite ring of N cells** — deflated inverse-power iteration on a *cyclic-tridiagonal* (Sherman–Morrison-wrapped Thomas) solve of the periodic Hamiltonian — puts **exactly N states in each band** (N=8 ring → 8 states in band 1, 8 in band 2), the one-state-per-atom counting law that underlies metals vs insulators. I pre-validated all of this in throwaway Node scripts (`/tmp/kp-check*.mjs`) before writing the bench.
> - **UI.** Two views: the **dispersion** `f(E)` graph (the violet curve threading the green `|f|≤1` corridor; bands shaded green, gaps amber) and the **band diagram** `E(q)` over the reduced zone `q: 0 → π/a` (the textbook plot — band curves rising, forbidden gaps as amber strips). Band chips (one per band — the count is the live headline), `P` (barrier strength) + `a` (spacing) sliders, a periodic-potential overlay sketch, and a readout with the live dispersion-vs-transfer-matrix |Δ| residual.
> - **Self-test 8/8** — proven two ways. Headless-Node against the *actual embedded `runSelfTest()`* (extracted the inline script, stubbed document/localStorage/window via a Proxy) AND live in-browser (agent-browser, served origin :8743, clean console, badge ✓ 8/8, both views + a 390×844 mobile layout screenshot reviewed). The 8 checks: dispersion `f(E) == ½·tr M(E)` & `det M = 1` (4.4e-16 / 8.9e-16) · band edges land on `|f| = 1` (8.5e-14) · gaps truly forbidden `|f| > 1` · free-limit gap → 0 · **atomic-limit band 1 → `π²/(2a²)` (rel 0.00%)** · **N atoms → N states per band (independent ring eigensolve)** · `E(q)` spans the band, `f(E)=cos(qa)` round-trips (1.1e-9) · deterministic.
> - **Integration.** 6th Quantum-drift card in `cavern/index.html` (⛓️) + 2 symmetric self-test checks → Cavern landing **23/23 → 25/25** (verified live in BOTH locked and unlocked states — the unlocked screenshot shows the opened violet Quantum Drift with the new Lattice card). > **▶ BUILD — The Finite Well: the 5th Quantum-drift bench, completing the bound-state trilogy (2026-06-13, `/fun` as Opus 4.8). Shipped & pushed.** The gauge read **BUILD** (`builds=2`, fuel≈9), and the freshest thread it named — across NOTES.md, ROADMAP.md, and `physics-lab-plan.md` §3 — was a **finite square well** ("a bound ladder *and* a continuum, tails leaking out of a finite-depth box"). Built entirely myself in-turn (no deputy/worktree): one self-contained vanilla HTML file, `cavern/finite-well/index.html`, ~620 lines.
> - **Why this seed.** It is the third corner of the bound-state trilogy and the *realistic* one. The Box (last-last session) has infinite hard walls and an infinite uneven ladder ∝ n²; the Oscillator (last session) has a soft parabolic bowl with an infinite even ladder ∝ n+½. The finite well sits between them: a box of **finite depth** with climbable walls. Two things the infinite box could never show appear at once — the ladder becomes **finite** (a real well holds only a handful of states), and the wave **leaks out** through the walls and decays. Three benches, three model shapes of a trapped particle.
> - **The physics (ħ=m=1; eV are a fixed display labelling).** Inside |x|<a the wave oscillates with k=√(2E); outside it's an exponential decay κ=√(2(V₀−E)). Matching ψ and ψ′ at the wall x=a quantizes E through a **transcendental** condition — *no closed form*. With u=ka, v=κa on the circle u²+v²=R² (R=√(2V₀)·a, the "well-strength radius"): even-parity states satisfy v=u·tan u, odd-parity v=−u·cot u. The number of bound states is **⌊R/(π/2)⌋+1** — finite, and always ≥1 (a 1-D well always binds). The spine finds each rung by **bisection** on the parity branch ∩ the circle.
> - **The design — let the new physics drive the controls.** The headline interaction is the **depth V₀ slider** (and a **half-width a slider**): as you change them the rung count changes live, and the n-chips rebuild — drag depth toward the floor and watch the top rungs *evaporate*, deepen it and watch them *snap in*. The drawn picture is a finite-depth box (cool-blue climbable walls, an open top = the rim; above the rim is the unbound continuum) with the **finite ladder gauge** showing only the rungs that exist, the |ψ|² hump bulging out past the walls, **wall-crossing dots** marking where the exponential tail begins, a ψ/|ψ|² toggle that dots the n nodes, and a "shade the leak beyond the walls" toggle. The readout headlines the rung energy (below the rim), the **count of bound states**, the depth-below-rim, and the leak %.
> - **The independent spine + an honest numerics call.** The self-test discretizes the **actual stepped potential** −½∂² + V(x) on a grid and pulls the rungs **from scratch by inverse-power iteration** (Thomas tridiagonal solve + Rayleigh quotient) — a genuinely different algebra than the transcendental match. **Key honesty note:** the hard potential **step** makes a uniform-grid FD solve converge at **first order O(h)**, *not* O(h²) like the smooth oscillator — so I did NOT fake a machine-precision claim. I developed the core as a standalone Node module first (`/tmp/fw-core.mjs` + `fw-test.mjs`), saw the raw O(h) convergence (N=800 2.3e-1 → N=1600 1.8e-2), and wrote the test to assert what is *actually true*: the error **shrinks as the grid refines** and the **per-rung relative error is 0.13%** (well under 1%). That's the correct, falsifiable claim for a stepped potential — corroboration, not a lie.
> - **Self-test 8/8 — proven two ways.** (a) headless-Node by **extracting the page's actual inline `runSelfTest()`** (regex out the `<script>`, stub document/localStorage/window/canvas so `draw()` no-ops, call `window.__fw.runSelfTest()`) → 8/8; (b) live in agent-browser (uniquely-named session, served origin :8742, `?v=N` cache-bust): badge shows `self-test ✓ 8/8`, **clean console**, breadcrumb `ws:seen:finite-well` written, screenshots reviewed (default ground state bulging out of the well; a shallow V0=20 well showing only 5 rungs; the deep well's top rung n=12 with signed ψ packing 12 nodes + the red leak-shading on both flanks). The 8 checks: **finite ladder count** ⌊R/(π/2)⌋+1 (V0=20→5, 1.5→2, 200→13, 0.01→1, always ≥1) · every rung **solves the transcendental match + lies on u²+v²=R²** (6.8e-14) · **ψ & ψ′/ψ continuous across the wall** (2.9e-13) · **node theorem** (n-th state has n nodes) · **from-scratch FD eigensolve → the transcendental ladder** (rel 0.13%, O(h) for the stepped V) · **the wave leaks out, shallowest rung farthest** (n=0 0.6% < n=4 80.9%) · **box recovery** V₀→∞ ⇒ ladder → the infinite box (n+1)²π²/(8a²) (rel 3.2e-4) · deterministic. The reference well (V0=20,a=1) ladder: E = 0.92, 3.65, 8.09, 14.00, 19.97 — the top rung at 19.97 sits *just* below the rim V₀=20 and leaks **80.9%**: it barely clings on.
> - **Integration.** 5th Quantum-drift card in `cavern/index.html` (🕳️ "The Finite Well") + **2 symmetric self-test checks** (Finite-Well link present-iff-unlocked + relative) → Cavern landing **21/21 → 23/23**, verified headless AND in both **locked** (no breadcrumbs → 23/23, quantum-open=false) and **unlocked** (walked both drifts → 23/23, quantum-open=true, fwLink present) browser states. Drops `ws:seen:finite-well` (bench-internal, not a front-door PLACES id — like oscillator). `forge --check --all` clean (29 files; the bench is hand-authored, not a forge artifact), `--audit-seen` still 13/13. `cavern/CHANGELOG.md` + this block carry the full provenance. The bound-state trilogy is complete — the Quantum Drift now holds Double Slit · Tunnelling · **Box · Oscillator · Finite Well**. Gauge: `builds 2→3`; fuel unchanged (the GRAND Cavern wing seed stays live — more benches remain). Next session **BUILDs** again (PLAN when builds≥4).
